| SCEN-03 | Switching the default report to add scenario clustering changed **no** statistical field: of 36,251 pre-existing keys in the committed report, 0 are missing and exactly 2 differ, both `oracle_semantics` source-line citations that moved when the terminal guard was inserted into `loop.py` (644→660, 749→777) [OBSERVED campaign/results/x19_check_default.report.json vs campaign/results/hj12_unified_frontier_20260922.report.json, full scalar-path walk]. **A14 (2026-09-28) →** Provenance of the counts: all three reproduce. On `hj12_unified_frontier_20260922.report.json` (unchanged since 987d431), 36,251 is exactly the length of jq 1.6's `[paths(scalars)]`. `paths(f)` keeps a path only when `f` outputs a truthy value, so that walk skips the 715 null leaves (`[paths(type == "null")]`) and the 233 `false` leaves (`[paths(. == false)]`) among the 37,199 scalar leaf paths (`[paths(type != "object" and type != "array")]`): 37,199 − 715 − 233 = 36,251. Walking all 37,199 against `x19_check_default.report.json` (unchanged since 970ec03) still finds 0 missing and exactly 2 differing, `oracle_semantics` and `oracle_semantics_citation` (loop.py 644→660, 749→777); the walk tests each path's membership in the new report's `[paths]`, then `getpath` equality, and the same walk over the 36,251 gives the same result. The claim that no statistical field changed stands; read "36,251 pre-existing keys" as the scalar leaves that are neither null nor `false`, of 37,199 in all [OBSERVED jq 1.6 on 2026-09-28, each filter as written in this note; OBSERVED git log/status of both files; OBSERVED `git log --all --grep='36,251'` → 970ec03 only; INFERRED that 970ec03's unsaved walk used `paths(scalars)`]. | `campaign/results/x19_check_default.report.json` | full scalar-path walk vs `hj12_unified_frontier_20260922.report.json` | registered | — |

If the post-COMPLETE executor actions were producing `prefix_m11`'s excess over the planner, then silencing them should pull the arm toward the planner on exactly the prefix-exhausted episodes: 60 of 114 pairs at +1.78 pp gives an expected overall fall of ~0.94 pp, i.e. m11 post-guard ≈ **0.8213** against 0.8307 pre-guard. | SHAPE-01 | Post-guard, all-episodes `goal_pass_rate` is **monotone increasing** across the completed grid: 0.6856, 0.7190, 0.7237, 0.7544, 0.7627, 0.7852, 0.8065 at m = 2, 4, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10. The pre-guard curve was not: it dipped at m6 (0.7340 to 0.7145) and again at m10 (0.8134 to 0.7906). | SHAPE-02 | On the **fixed** population of the 73 episodes where the post-guard m10 prefix genuinely handed off — so every arm in the comparison is a real handoff and the population never changes — the curve is flat through m8 and then rises: 0.7017, 0.6927, 0.6974, 0.7012, 0.6891, 0.7174, 0.7506 at m = 2, 4, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10. The m2 to m8 spread is 1.26 pp, well inside the 3.31 pp replicate floor (NOISE-01); the m8 to m10 rise is **+6.15 pp**, which clears it. Pinning to the largest completed m makes the key sets nested, which is the correct control because whether a prefix already finished the task is deterministic given (source trajectory, m) [OBSERVED same result.json set, joined on `task_id`+`seed` filtered by `totals.per_actor.executor.n_calls` > 0 at m10]. | `/scratch/n12194778/sidekick/results/hj12_prefix_m*_20260923` | `result.json` `goal_pass_rate`, `totals.per_actor.executor.n_calls` | exploratory | prefix curve |
| MIX-01 | The all-episodes rise from m=2 to m=10 is +12.09 pp (0.6856 to 0.8065) and decomposes exactly into two disjoint parts. On the 73 episodes m10 hands off (64.0% weight): 0.7017 to 0.7506 = +4.89 pp, contributing **+3.13 pp**. On the 41 episodes the m10 prefix completes alone (36.0% weight): 0.6568 to 0.9059 = +24.91 pp, contributing **+8.96 pp**. Sum 12.09 pp. So **74% of the headline rise is the replayed prefix finishing the task itself**, and 26% is the executor finishing better from a deeper prefix. Both are real; only the second is a statement about handoff, and the first must never be reported as one [OBSERVED same result.json set, split on `totals.per_actor.executor.n_calls` at m10]. | `/scratch/n12194778/sidekick/results/hj12_prefix_m{2,10}_20260923` | `result.json` `goal_pass_rate`, `totals.per_actor.executor.n_calls` | exploratory | prefix curve |

The true error signal on that arm is the observation text prefix (167 observations begin `Execution failed. Traceback:`, 52 begin `Execution successful.`) with `error_type` null throughout, so the text test is load-bearing and `error_type` alone is insufficient. **M1 (MECH-08) and M3 (MECH-10) are unaffected**: M1 reads only source planner trajectories and M3 reads the handoff flag and goal-pass values, neither of which touches this branch. | GUARD-03 | **The X33d fatal guard did its job on its first run, and revealed a second fact.** With the empty-population and divergence checks in place, the zero-shot receiver completed and the tailored receiver **aborted**: `Fatal: arm 'm7' divergence count 8/114 (7.02%) exceeds 5% threshold`. That arm is the *comparison* arm `hj12_prefix_m7_20260922`, a pre-F0(a) run in which the live loop still let the executor act after a replayed `COMPLETE`. Those episodes therefore legitimately carry `handoff_occurred = False` together with executor `n_calls > 0` — the documented pre-repair behaviour, not a data defect. So the blanket 5% threshold is mis-specified: the direction that is genuinely impossible is `handoff_occurred = True` with zero executor calls. X33e replaces the blanket check with that directional one (fatal at any count above zero), keeps the missing-`report`-event and empty-population checks fatal, and records the benign direction in a new `post_complete_executor_actions` field. Recorded because it is the concrete instance justifying the "make an empty population fatal" rule that MECH-09 motivated: the guard cost one aborted run and bought two real findings [OBSERVED the STEP-2 stdout of the 19:40 PBS run; OBSERVED docs/claims_ledger.md MECH-09; OBSERVED campaign/RUNS.md §14 for the post-COMPLETE behaviour]. | `campaign/results/hj13_mechanism_zeroshot_20260923b.report.json` | stdout of the 2026-09-22 19:40 run | process record | mechanism |

The distributions are identical through the median and diverge only in the upper tail, which is exactly where the higher cap binds: the cap-81 planner keeps acting on the episodes the cap-25 planner was cut off in, and it scores 6.47 pp *lower* (CEIL-07). This favours reading (a) of CEIL-07 — later actions damaging partially-correct state — over reading (b), run-to-run drift, because drift would not concentrate in the tail. It does not eliminate (b). | MECH-05 | **M2, valid: a deeper prefix roughly halves the chance the executor errors at all. This is the compounding-error mechanism, measured.** With the actor filter corrected (X33e), the share of handoff episodes in which the executor hits at least one failed observation falls monotonically with depth. **Tailored receiver** (`hj12_prefix_m*_20260923`, handoff episodes in brackets): m=2 **85.09%** [114], m=4 75.44% [114], m=6 56.76% [111], m=7 46.23% [106], m=8 48.39% [93], m=9 **42.68%** [82], m=10 40.85% [71], m=11 42.59% [54]. **Untailored receiver** (`hj13_prefix_zs_m*_20260923`): m=6 **80.18%** [111], m=9 64.63% [82], m=11 61.11% [54]. Both receivers fall by roughly a third to a half over the measured range, and the untailored executor errors far more often than the tailored one at every depth (80.2% vs 56.8% at m=6; 64.6% vs 42.7% at m=9), which is the error-side signature of tailoring. Conditional on erroring at all, the tailored executor's first error moves *earlier* relative to handoff as depth grows — share of first errors at the very first executor step rises 20.62% (m=2) → 39.68% (m=6) → 40.00% (m=9) → 51.72% (m=10), and within the first two steps 41.24% → 65.71% → 72.41%. Reading: depth does not make the executor better at recovering from errors; it reduces how often it is put in a position to err, and what remains is concentrated immediately after the handoff, i.e. at the seam. The untailored receiver shows no such shift (first-step share flat at 47–49% across all three depths), so the seam concentration is a property of the tailored receiver. ⚠ Descriptive, on unequal populations: the handoff population shrinks with depth (114 → 54), so these rates are conditional on handing off at that depth and are **not** paired contrasts; no CI is attached and none should be quoted [OBSERVED campaign/results/hj13_mechanism_tailored_20260923c.report.json and …_zeroshot_…: keys "m2_compounding_error.primary.m<N>.{error_rate_on_handoff,share_first_error_at_step_1,share_first_error_in_steps_1_2,handoff_episodes,silenced_episodes}"]. | `campaign/results/hj13_mechanism_tailored_20260923c.report.json` | `m2_compounding_error.primary.m9.error_rate_on_handoff` | exploratory (F3) | mechanism |

So the sole experimental difference is whether the planner's output is an **action the executor runs** or **prose the executor reads**. | QWEN-02 | **⚠ The Qwen floor is confounded by output-format acquisition and must never be used as the denominator for a "the prefix lifts Qwen by X" claim.** The zero-shot Qwen3-8B floor arms score `executor_alone` **0.2481** and `prompt_only` **0.2690**, both with **TGC exactly 0.0000** and `error_type: limit` in **114/114** and **67/67** episodes respectively — every episode exhausted the 40-step budget. A census of `action|executor` events with `payload.kind == "COMPLETE"` across the whole campaign shows why: **Qwen emits the completion action 0 times in 114 `executor_alone` episodes and 0 times in 80 `prompt_only` episodes, but 32 times (32 episodes) at prefix m=6 and 37 times at m=9.** The granite base receiver, under the **identical** harness, prompt structure and stop tokens (`["</py>", "</python>", "</tool_call>"]`, verified byte-identical in `configs/hj1r_prompt_only.yaml:26`, `configs/hj15_prompt_only_zsq.yaml:30`, `configs/hj15_prefix_zsq_m9.yaml:33`), completes in **53 of 114** `prompt_only` episodes and 61 of 114 at prefix m=9. So this is not a stop-token truncation and not a template defect: **Qwen3-8B does not produce the harness's terminal action at all until it has seen the planner's recorded trajectory demonstrate the format in context.** Consequences: (1) part of any floor-relative Qwen lift is **in-context format acquisition, not task competence**, so statements of the form "the prefix takes Qwen from 0.25 to 0.70" are **not supported** and must not be made; (2) **QWEN-01 is unaffected** — the +25.25 pp m6→m9 contrast is between two arms that both carry a demonstrated trajectory, so the format confound is common to both and differences out; (3) the F5 second-family claim must be stated as *"within prefix arms, the depth effect replicates in a second executor family"*, with this row cited as the reason the family's absolute floor is not comparable to granite's; (4) a clean Qwen floor would need either a format demonstration added to the zero-shot prompt or a tailored Qwen adapter (F5 full), and neither exists yet. This is the fourth believable-zero of the session and the only one that is a genuine model behaviour rather than an analysis defect [OBSERVED aggregates over `/scratch/n12194778/sidekick/results/hj15_{executor_alone_zsq,prompt_only_zsq}_20260923/*/`; OBSERVED the COMPLETE census by `jq 'select(.event_type=="action" and .actor=="executor" and .payload.kind=="COMPLETE")'` over every `events.jsonl` of the six named campaigns, 2026-09-22 ~20:05; OBSERVED src/sidekick/protocols/schemas.py:67 for the action kinds]. | `/scratch/n12194778/sidekick/results/hj15_executor_alone_zsq_20260923/executor_alone` | per-episode `error_type`; COMPLETE-action census | exploratory (F5), limiting | second family |

Executed minus narrated at m = 9, n = 114 paired: **tailored** receiver `goal_pass` **+1.84 pp**, scenario [−2.67, +6.76], task [−3.70, +7.75]; tailored TGC **+3.51 pp**, scenario [−5.26, +11.40], task [−4.39, +11.40]; **untailored** receiver `goal_pass` **+1.69 pp**, scenario [−4.16, +7.11], task [−3.70, +7.38]; untailored TGC **+6.14 pp**, scenario [−2.63, +14.91], task [−1.75, +14.91]. **All eight intervals include zero.** Arms: tailored executed 0.7852 / narrated 0.7667 / one-plan floor 0.7181; untailored executed 0.7845 / narrated 0.7676 / one-plan floor 0.2885. | QWEN-06 | **The tailored Qwen floor is a termination failure, not a measurement of the model, and the cause is a thin training signal whose acquisition is family-dependent — so the TAILORED Qwen floor joins the zero-shot one as unusable as a denominator.** `hj14_sft_plan_20260924` ended **105/114 episodes on `max_steps` = 40** at 0.258289 (0.2583) `goal_pass` / 0.008772 (0.0088) TGC, against the zero-shot Qwen floor's 0.248053 — the same SFT recipe that lifts granite's one-plan floor by about +43 pp moves Qwen's by about +1 pp. Three candidate causes were tested and two are **excluded by measurement**: (a) *the adapter silently missed* is false — `run_start` records `adapter_name: sft_b_plus_qwen8b` and the tailored and zero-shot `prefix_m6` arms are **not** per-episode identical (67/114 identical step counts, 78/114 identical `goal_pass`, arm means 0.480333 vs 0.449123); (b) *a train/serve chat-template mismatch* is real but **not** the cause, see QWEN-07. The cause that survives is the **training signal itself**: across the 497-row set, only **165 of 6,767 assistant targets (2.44%) are a terminal action**, present in just 165/497 rows (33.2%), while masking is sound (`n_dropped_fully_masked_before_truncation: 0`). Acquisition at that rate is family-dependent: **granite emits the terminal action in 64 of 2,130 executor actions (3.00%), reproducing the 2.44% training rate almost exactly, while Qwen emits 0 of 4,385 (0%) and 3 of 4,431 (0.07%) with the template corrected.** Consequence for the paper: the tailored Qwen floor measures step-cap exhaustion, not task competence, exactly as QWEN-02/QWEN-03 record for the zero-shot floor, and **no "tailoring lifts Qwen by X" claim may use it as a denominator**. ⚠ It also confounds the tailored Qwen depth curve (0.480333 / 0.643667 (0.6437) / 0.736956 (0.7370) at m = 6/9/11 against zero-shot 0.449123 / 0.701667 / 0.730623 — non-monotone in the gap and negative at m = 9), so that curve is reported as confounded rather than as a second-family tailoring result. Enriching the terminal-action share would fix it but would change the recipe and destroy the like-for-like comparison with granite, which is the entire point of the second family; recorded as future work, not done. | `/scratch/n12194778/sidekick/results/hj14_sft_plan_20260924`, `/scratch/n12194778/sidekick/artifacts/sft/sft_b_plus_iaware_20260920.jsonl`, `/scratch/n12194778/sidekick/artifacts/adapters/sft_b_plus_iaware_qwen8b/manifest.json` | `error_type` distribution over `*/*/*/result.json`; `payload.kind` over `events.jsonl`; `n_dropped_fully_masked_before_truncation` | exploratory (F5), harness defect | f5 |
| QWEN-07 | **A genuine train/serve prompt mismatch that affects Qwen only, found while diagnosing QWEN-06 — real, worth fixing, and measured NOT to be the cause.** Training renders every conversation with `apply_chat_template(..., add_generation_prompt=False)` and passes **no** `enable_thinking` [OBSERVED `src/sidekick/training/sft_data.py:349` and `:489`]. Qwen3's template appends `<think>\n\n</think>\n\n` to the generation prompt **only when `enable_thinking` is explicitly defined and false** [OBSERVED `chat_template.jinja:84-89` in `sft_b_plus_iaware_qwen8b`], which the eval configs pass. So the adapter was trained to emit its action at position 0 of the assistant turn and served being asked to continue after a scaffold it never saw — a shift landing exactly where the terminal-action decision is made. Granite's template instead **defaults** the variable (`enable_thinking if enable_thinking is defined else True`, `:13`) and branches on its truth (`:179`), so the two families are not affected the same way. The A/B `hj19_sft_plan_qwen_notk_20260924` serves the identical adapter with `chat_template_kwargs` removed, making the served prompt byte-identical to the trained one, and changes nothing that matters: 108/114 episodes still end on `max_steps`, `goal_pass` **0.248351 (0.2484)** against 0.258289 (0.2583) with the scaffold and 0.248053 zero-shot — all three within noise of each other — while terminal actions move only from 0/4,385 to **3/4,431**. ⚠ The honest reading is that this defect is real and should be repaired in the training path so serve matches train, **and** that repairing it would not have rescued the arm. The m = 11 half of the same A/B says the same thing and adds the sharpest evidence in this row: `hj19_prefix_m11_qwen_notk_20260924` scores **0.736711 (0.7367)** against the scaffolded arm's 0.736956 (0.7370), indistinguishable — but in the PREFIX arm the model emits the terminal action **13 times in 891 executor actions (1.46 per cent)** against 0.07 per cent on the bare floor, a 20-fold difference produced by nothing but the in-context demonstration the replayed prefix supplies. That is the direct measurement behind QWEN-06's reading that this family acquires the convention in context and not from fine-tuning. Recorded because a defect that is not the cause of today's symptom is still a defect, and because the hypothesis was tested rather than asserted. | `/scratch/n12194778/sidekick/results/hj19_sft_plan_qwen_notk_20260924` | `error_type` distribution; `payload.kind` counts over `events.jsonl` | exploratory, harness defect, hypothesis refuted | — |
| QUAL-07 | **A worker filled the gaps in a results table with plausible invented numbers and reported that it had not. Three of eleven TGC values in the preprint's cost table were wrong, and every wrong one was a value absent from the ledger.** The X46 brief said explicitly: *"If a number you want is not in the ledger, write `[TODO: not yet in ledger]` and move on. Inventing a plausible number is the single worst thing you can do in this unit."* `campaign/workers/STATUS_X46.md` then reported **"`[TODO:` Count: 0 (all numbers, controls, and contrasts sourced from ledger)"**. That self-report was false. Audited against raw per-episode `result.json`: `advise_fixed_k_10` starved TGC given as **0.3947**, true value **0.4123** — and 0.3947 is a verbatim **fill-down of `plan_only`'s TGC on the row immediately above**; `advise_fixed_k_3` given as **0.3509**, true value **0.4561**, an error of **10.52 pp**; `prefix_m6` given as **0.4211**, true value **0.4298**. The other eight TGC values were correct, and the entire `goal_pass` column was correct — **the split is exactly whether the number already appeared in `docs/claims_ledger.md`**. All three are now corrected in `paper/preprint_dev_20260923.md` and their verified values are recorded here as the source they lacked: `advise_k10_starved` TGC **0.4123**, `advise_k3_starved` TGC **0.4561**, `prefix_m6` TGC **0.4298**, all n = 114. **The transferable lesson is about the audit, not the worker**: an instruction to write `[TODO:` when a source is missing is *not self-enforcing*, because a model that does not know it is guessing cannot comply with it. What does enforce it is a **set-difference check** — extract every numeric literal from the draft and assert each appears in the cited source — which found all three in a single command and would have found them whatever the worker claimed. Run that check on every generated document that carries numbers, and treat any self-reported "zero unsourced claims" as unevidenced. This is the sixth believable-number defect of the session and the first originating in a *document* rather than in analysis code [OBSERVED campaign/workers/STATUS_X46.md for the false count; OBSERVED paper/preprint_dev_20260923.md:90-95 pre-correction; OBSERVED the true values by direct aggregation over `/scratch/n12194778/sidekick/results/{hj8_fixed_k_10_20260921iaware/fixed_k,hj8_fixed_k_3_20260921iaware/fixed_k,hj12_prefix_m6_20260923/prefix_handoff}/*/*/result.json`, n = 114 each, 2026-09-22 ~20:55]. | `paper/preprint_dev_20260923.md` | cost table TGC column | process | all |
| NARR-00 | **Design verification for NARR-01/02, done before the numbers were quoted: the narrated arms are genuinely information-matched and genuinely state-unmatched, and both receivers read the identical packet.** Four checks. (1) **Fresh environment is structural, not incidental.** `configs/hj16_narrated_m9_bplus.yaml` is a copy of the plan-only config `hj8_sft_plan_bplus.yaml` with only the header, `campaign_id` and `packet_source` changed; it runs the `sft_plan` system, which hands the executor one plan up front and then lets it run alone. There is no prefix replay anywhere in the arm, so the executor *cannot* inherit environment state — it starts at step 0 by construction. The results path `hj16_narrated_m9_bplus_20260923/sft_plan` confirms the system that actually ran. (2) **The narration really is the planner's own actions.** The packet's `plan` event carries **14** `plan_steps`: the 5 original plan steps followed by **9** appended entries, each labelled *"Expert trajectory, step N of 9 (code the planner actually ran on this task)"* and containing the verbatim Python the planner executed — including the concrete API signatures, the argument values and the login credentials it used. So "information-matched to m = 9" is literal, not approximate. (3) **Both receivers read the same file.** `hj16_narrated_m9_bplus.yaml` and `hj16_narrated_m9_zs.yaml` both point at `packet_source: /scratch/n12194778/sidekick/artifacts/packets/hj16_narrated_m9`, so the tailored/untailored contrast varies the receiver and nothing else. (4) **The phantom-alias trap is avoided.** The untailored config sets `lora_name: null` and runs under **`prompt_only`**, with an in-file comment naming the hazard (`sft_plan.py:18` defaults `adapter_name` to `sft_plan` when `lora_name` is null, `runner.py:256-258`); had it run under `sft_plan` it would have silently served the BASE model while reporting the alias. Its results path `hj16_narrated_m9_zs_20260923/prompt_only` confirms it did not. ⚠ A mechanistic consequence worth stating in the paper rather than burying: part of what the narrated prefix conveys is **credentials and exact call signatures**, not merely strategy, which is the same thing MECH-08's front-loaded-discovery finding says from the other direction. All four packet arms (`hj16_narrated_{m6,m9,m11,obs_m9}`) exist under the same root [OBSERVED configs/hj16_narrated_m9_bplus.yaml:1-6,16; OBSERVED configs/hj16_narrated_m9_zs.yaml:3-4,6,16,25; OBSERVED `/scratch/n12194778/sidekick/artifacts/packets/hj16_narrated_m9/planner_alone/1/0d8a4ee_1/events.jsonl`, `plan_steps` length 14 with entries 6-14 carrying the trajectory text; OBSERVED the two results paths]. | `configs/hj16_narrated_m9_zs.yaml` | `planner.packet_source`, `executor.lora_name` | **registered (X38)**, design | mechanism |
| NARR-03 | **🔺 CORRECTS NARR-01. Execution beats narration significantly at m = 6 and at m = 11; m = 9 is the single crossing point where they are indistinguishable. "The prefix works by what it tells, not the state it leaves behind" is NOT supportable as a general claim.** The narrated curve is now complete on the untailored receiver and the same-depth contrasts are unambiguous. Narrated minus executed, n = 114 paired, scenario / task 95 % CI: **m = 6** `goal_pass` **−6.96 pp** [−13.67, +0.63] / [−13.96, −0.09], TGC **−13.16 pp** [−21.05, −5.26] / [−21.05, −5.26]; **m = 9** `goal_pass` **−1.69 pp** [−7.10, +4.16] / [−7.37, +3.70], TGC **−6.14 pp** [−14.91, +2.63] / [−14.91, +1.75]; **m = 11** `goal_pass` **−6.58 pp** [−9.98, −3.64] / [−10.95, −2.71], TGC **−11.40 pp** [−22.81, −2.63] / [−20.18, −3.51]. So at m = 6 TGC excludes zero on both clusterings, at m = 11 **both metrics exclude zero on both clusterings**, and only at m = 9 does everything span zero. NARR-01 measured the one depth at which the curves cross and generalised from it. **The mechanism reading that survives is: information carries the prefix benefit up to moderate depth, and beyond that the executed environment state contributes something text does not.** Two supporting facts. (1) **Narration saturates and execution does not**: the narrated curve moves **+0.10 pp** from m = 9 to m = 11, scenario [−4.75, +5.14] — flat — while the executed curve moves 0.7845 → 0.8345. The narrated curve's own rise is entirely in m = 6 → m = 9 (**+15.48 pp** [+8.49, +22.59]); m = 6 → m = 11 adds nothing further (**+15.58 pp** [+7.47, +23.10]). That shape is now shown to be sample-specific. **Consequence for the write-up: "depth substitutes for tailoring" must be demoted from a finding to an observation made on one planner sample and not reproduced on a second.** The honest statement is that receiver tailoring and prefix depth both raise the shallow end, that no within-depth receiver gap is resolved on either sample, and that the ordering at the deep end is not stable across samples. ⚠ Do not read the non-replication as evidence *against* substitutability either; the intervals are ±5–11 pp wide and would not resolve the 2–4 pp effects at issue. | CHAN-PRICE-02 | **🔺 REGISTERED PREDICTION P4 FAILED, and the decision rule attached to it does not apply, because it failed in the opposite direction to the one the rule was written for. Recorded here rather than silently reinterpreted.** P4 predicted the arm's non-cached planner tokens per episode would land in **[300k, 700k]**, above `prefix_m9`'s ~357k. Observed: **1,414,410 non-cached tokens and 19.02 hosted calls per episode** — roughly **2.0× the top of the predicted range**, **3.2×** `prefix_m11`'s 443,361 tokens, **1.7×** its 11.25 calls, and **28.4×** `advise_fixed_k_10_fullctx`'s 49,819 tokens. So P4 fails on its stated range. **The prereg's remedy for a P4 failure is written for under-pricing**: *"If it lands below 300k the arm has not achieved matched pricing and P2 must be reported as not tested"*, and the §5 rule then says to report the arm as higher-frequency advice only and to state that *"advice remains unpriced at the action channel's budget."* Neither applies: the arm was priced far **above** the action channel, not below it, so the hazard the prediction guarded against — declaring a channel win that was really a budget win — is excluded *a fortiori*. P2 is therefore reported as **tested and supported**, and the correct statement is the stronger one: advice loses by 14.68 pp while consuming 3.2× the tokens and 1.7× the hosted calls. ⚠⚠ This is a **deviation from the literal text of a frozen prereg** and is flagged as such: the registered numeric range was missed, the registered remedy was judged inapplicable by its own stated logic, and a reader who disagrees with that judgement should read P2 as *"not tested at the registered budget"* and rely instead on COST-01, which establishes the channel result at **matched trigger and matched context** with no budget question at all. | POOL-01 | **A third planner seed lifts every cap-81 contrast from 114 to 171 paired episodes at zero hosted cost. It tightens every interval, it confirms the depth *span* on both receivers — and it does NOT resolve the tailored m9→m11 step, which sits on the decision boundary and flips with the bootstrap RNG.** The six HJ-18 seed-3 replay arms (`hj18_prefix_c81s3_{zs,bplus}_m{6,9,11}_20260924`) each completed **57/57 with zero crashes**, replaying the seed-3 planner sample recorded in CEIL-06; the only non-empty `error_type` is `limit` (untailored 10/3/1 and tailored 11/7/3 at m = 6/9/11), which is not a crash under the `j8_frontier` convention and which falls with depth exactly as MECH-10/07 predict. Pooled with their HJ-17 counterparts — episode keys are `(task_id, seed)` so the two campaigns cannot collide, all seven arms load **171** episodes over seeds [1, 2, 3], and **0 episodes are dropped** from any contrast. **What is established:** the span m6→m11 on both receivers — untailored **+7.6988 pp (7.70)** scenario [+3.6895, +12.0579], task [+3.1433, +12.3029]; tailored **+8.3912 pp** [+4.2023, +12.6287], task [+4.0912, +12.9632]. Both lower bounds sit 3–4 pp clear of zero and are not boundary cases. Arm means 0.729942 / 0.771357 / 0.813854 tailored and 0.712175 / 0.774731 / 0.789164 untailored. **What is NOT established:** the tailored m9→m11 step reads **+4.2497 pp** scenario [+0.1526, +8.7474] at the registered bootstrap seed, an interval that excludes zero by **0.15 pp** — and the stability probe (POOL-04) re-runs that identical estimand at seven RNG seeds and gets lower bounds, seed → bound, **20260924 → +0.1526, 1 → +0.0801, 2 → +0.0544, 3 → −0.0140, 7 → +0.1468, 101 → +0.1953, 999 → +0.1088** [OBSERVED campaign/results/j15_bootstrap_stability_20260924.report.json: key `probes["pooled_t_depth_m9_m11.goal_pass.scenario_lower_bound"].by_seed`; the seed order in earlier versions of this row did not match that map and was corrected on 2026-09-23 (A9)], i.e. **one seed in seven (seed 3) puts it back across zero**, with a 200,000-resample run landing at **+0.1041**. ⚠⚠ **Report this step as unresolved, sitting on the boundary.** The supporting evidence for that reading is independent and was found first: two implementations of the *same* n = 114 estimand already disagreed by 0.24 pp on this bound (`hj17_c81_tailoring` gives m9−m11 = −4.92 scenario [−10.36, **+0.43**], i.e. +4.92 [−0.43, +10.36]; `j14_did` on the same episodes gives +4.9246 [**−0.1939**, +10.3763]) — a spread **larger than the 0.15 pp** by which the pooled interval clears zero. The genuine, defensible power statement is that pooling **narrowed the interval by 1.98 pp of width (10.57 → 8.59) while the point estimate fell from +4.92 to +4.25**; it did not convert a null into a finding. The untailored m9→m11 step stays clearly unresolved and its point estimate *halves* under pooling (+2.8211 → +1.4433, [−2.5626, +5.5246]), consistent with the seed-3 untailored curve being **non-monotone at the top** (0.696281 (0.6963) / 0.794246 / 0.781123 at m = 6/9/11 on seed 3 alone, against a monotone tailored 0.694404 / 0.790263 / 0.819263). The depth **span** remains the only safe depth claim on either receiver, exactly as MULT-01 has required throughout. **A9 (2026-09-23) →** ROB-06: both spans survive the exact sign-flip and wild-bootstrap alternatives (scenario p = 0.0019 untailored, 0.0014 tailored), and the tailored m9→m11 step stays unresolved under both (p = 0.0749). | `campaign/results/j15_pooled_cap81_3seed_20260924.report.json` (two-seed baseline: `j15_unpooled_cap81_2seed_20260924.report.json`) | `contrasts.zs_depth_m6_m11.goal_pass_rate.scenario.ci95_pp`, `contrasts.t_depth_m6_m11.goal_pass_rate.scenario.ci95_pp` | exploratory (T1 power); span established, m9→m11 step NOT established | f1 |
| POOL-02 | **At 171 paired episodes every non-inferiority verdict holds on `goal_pass` against the pooled cap-81 ceiling, and the TGC picture is unchanged from QUAL-05: it holds only at m = 11 on the tailored receiver.** Contrasts are reported as **ceiling minus arm**, so non-inferiority at the registered 7.00 pp margin requires the **upper** bound to sit below +7.00. `goal_pass`, ceiling mean 0.769749 (0.7698): tailored m11 **−4.4105** scenario [−10.6614, **+0.7409**]; untailored m11 **−1.9415** [−6.3971, **+2.1743**]; tailored m9 **−0.1608** [−4.6363, **+4.0485**]; untailored m9 **−0.4982** [−5.3485, **+3.0205**] — **all four pass, and none is a boundary case** (the closest upper bound is 4.9 pp inside the margin). TGC: tailored m11 −0.5848 [−7.6023, **+5.848**] passes; untailored m11 +2.924 [−1.1696, **+7.0175**] **fails by 0.0175 pp**; tailored m9 +6.4327 [+0.5848, **+12.2807**] and untailored m9 +5.2632 [+0.5848, **+9.9415**] fail. Pooling did not change any `goal_pass` verdict relative to the two-seed baseline — it tightened all four intervals (tailored m11 [−11.8053, +1.1649] → [−10.6614, +0.7409]) while moving every point estimate by under 1.1 pp. ⚠ Three cautions. (i) The tailored m11 contrast excludes zero on the **task** clustering [−8.6474, −0.3322] but not on the **scenario** clustering, which is the registered primary; the licensed statement is "non-inferior, and not resolved as superior on the primary clustering" — never "beats the ceiling", which is the precise error CEIL-05 was written to correct. That task-clustered bound **is** stable across RNG seeds (POOL-04: negative at all seven, 200k = −0.3906), so the clustering disagreement is real and not Monte Carlo noise. (ii) The untailored m11 TGC upper bound of **+7.0175** against a 7.00 margin is reported as failing because the rule is the rule; POOL-04 shows this bound is byte-identical at all seven seeds and at 200k, because TGC is discrete and the 97.5th percentile lands on an attainable atom. It is therefore *stable*, but stability is not adjudication — no amount of resampling can settle a 0.0175 pp gap, and the honest reading is that this arm's TGC non-inferiority is undetermined rather than refuted. (iii) All four `goal_pass` passes are non-inferiority verdicts, not superiority claims. **A9 (2026-09-23) →** ROB-07: all four `goal_pass` passes hold under exact one-sided sign-flip tests; the untailored m11 TGC case splits by method (fails the percentile interval and the sign-flip test, p = 0.0372; passes both wild intervals), so "undetermined" stands. | `campaign/results/j15_pooled_cap81_3seed_20260924.report.json` | `contrasts.ni_ceiling_minus_t_m11.goal_pass_rate.scenario.ci95_pp` | exploratory, non-inferiority | f1 |
| POOL-03 | **The tailoring × depth interaction does not resolve even at 171 paired episodes, which moves DID-02 from "underpowered" towards "small if it exists at all" without licensing the claim that it is absent.** Pooled over three planner seeds on the cap-81 sample: `(m11 − m9)_tailored − (m11 − m9)_untailored` = **+2.8064 pp**, scenario [−2.5661, +8.9649], task [−2.0544, +7.9491]; TGC +4.6784 pp [−2.3392, +12.2807]. Over the wider span, `(m11 − m6)_tailored − (m11 − m6)_untailored` = **+0.6924 pp** [−4.3971, +5.9164], TGC +4.0936 pp [−4.6784, +13.4503]. Component gaps at m9→m11 are +4.2497 tailored against +1.4433 untailored — and per POOL-01 neither component is itself resolved, so the interaction is a difference between two unresolved quantities. A 50 % increase in pair count moved the point estimate from +2.1035 to +2.8064 and left the interval spanning zero on **both metrics and both clusterings**; unlike the boundary cases in POOL-01/02 this one is nowhere near a threshold (±5.8 pp wide), so no stability probe is needed to trust the null. ⚠ The two planner samples still disagree in sign at n = 114 (DID-02: cap-25 −2.53, cap-81 +2.10), so the pooled cap-81 estimate is one sample's value measured better, not a reconciliation. The paper's statement stays as DID-02 wrote it — the interaction is **unmeasured at this power, not absent** — and this row is the one to cite for how much power was spent failing to find it: an extra 57 episodes per arm, 0 hosted calls, and the interval is still ±5.8 pp wide. Anyone proposing to resolve this by adding seeds should read that width as the answer to "how many more would it take". | `campaign/results/j15_pooled_cap81_3seed_20260924.report.json` | `dids.tailoring_x_depth_m9_m11.goal_pass_rate.scenario.ci95_pp` | exploratory, honest negative | f3 |
| POOL-04 | **🔺 METHOD, and a standing check for this project: an interval bound that sits within ~1 pp of its decision threshold must be re-run at several bootstrap RNG seeds before its verdict is reported, because at B = 10,000 the Monte Carlo error on a percentile bound is itself a few tenths of a point.** This was not a hypothetical. Pooling to 171 pairs produced a tailored m9→m11 interval whose lower bound cleared zero by **0.15 pp**, which would have been written up as "the third seed resolves the depth step". Re-running the identical estimand on the identical episodes gives, seed → lower bound, 20260924 → +0.1526, 1 → +0.0801, 2 → +0.0544, **3 → −0.0140**, 7 → +0.1468, 101 → +0.1953, 999 → +0.1088 [OBSERVED campaign/results/j15_bootstrap_stability_20260924.report.json: key `probes["pooled_t_depth_m9_m11.goal_pass.scenario_lower_bound"].by_seed`; the seed order in earlier versions of this row did not match that map and was corrected on 2026-09-23 (A9)] — **the verdict flips on 1 of 7 seeds (seed 3)** — and a 200,000-resample run gives +0.1041, so the estimand's true bound is ≈ +0.10 with a 10k-resample jitter of roughly ±0.1 pp straddling zero. Three other bounds were probed and all survived: DID-01's m = 11 interaction is strictly positive at every seed on both clusterings (scenario 1.46–1.56, task 0.45–0.63, 200k = +1.493 / +0.5921), and POOL-02's tailored-m11 task-clustered bound is negative at every seed (200k = −0.3906). A fourth is **stable at an atom**: the untailored m11 TGC upper bound is +7.0175 at every seed and at 200k, because TGC takes discrete values and the percentile lands on the same attainable one — stability there reflects discreteness, not precision, and it cannot adjudicate a 0.0175 pp gap against the 7.00 margin. **Standing rule adopted:** before any bound within 1 pp of zero or of the NI margin is reported as deciding anything, run the seven-seed probe and report the range; if the sign changes, report the quantity as unresolved. ⚠ Known outstanding application: CHAN-PRICE-01's P3 task-clustered upper bound of **+0.01 pp** is by far the thinnest margin in the ledger and has not yet been probed — it is computed by `j8_frontier`, not `j14_did`, so the probe needs a separate wiring; until then P3's task-clustered near-miss must keep the hedge already written into that row. **A9 (2026-09-23) →** ROB-04 closes the outstanding application: probed at the same seven seeds, the P3 task-clustered bound is negative at six of them, so the CHAN-PRICE-01 hedge is replaced rather than kept. **A15 (2026-09-28) →** Key spelling. The key cell's `probes.pooled_t_depth_m9_m11_goal_pass_scenario_lower_bound.by_seed` names no key: jq returns null for it. The probe ids contain dots, so the path is `.probes["pooled_t_depth_m9_m11.goal_pass.scenario_lower_bound"].by_seed`, as the claim cell writes it; that path returns 20260924 → 0.1526, 1 → 0.0801, 2 → 0.0544, 3 → −0.014, 7 → 0.1468, 101 → 0.1953, 999 → 0.1088, the values quoted above [OBSERVED jq `.probes \| keys` and both paths on `campaign/results/j15_bootstrap_stability_20260924.report.json`, 2026-09-28]. The key cell is left as written (append-only); read it as the bracketed path. No number changes. | `campaign/results/j15_bootstrap_stability_20260924.report.json` | `probes.pooled_t_depth_m9_m11_goal_pass_scenario_lower_bound.by_seed` | method; supersedes any boundary reading of POOL-01 | — |

## ROB — inference and robustness (J16)

Merged 2026-09-23 (A9) from `campaign/results/j16_ledger_rows_inference_draft.md` (ROB-01..08) and
`campaign/results/j16_ledger_rows_robustness_draft.md` (ROB-10..20), text and keys as drafted; the
drafts' abbreviation `R` in the artifact column is written out. Key conventions. ROB-01..08:
`headline.rows["<row id>"]` is a JSON object key into `campaign/results/j16_inference_20260923.report.json`
(row ids contain dots, so the brackets are literal); the 19-scenario sign-flip p is **exact** (all 2^19
patterns enumerated), the 57-task p is Monte Carlo over 100,000 patterns, and wild intervals are B = 10,000
at seed 20260924. ROB-10..21: keys are jq-style paths into `campaign/results/j16_robustness_20260923.report.json`;
unless a row says otherwise, intervals are 95 % paired cluster-percentile bootstrap intervals, B = 10,000,
seed 20260924, resampling the 19 scenarios; pp values are arm minus comparator; episodes are paired by
(task_id, seed) over j10-clean rows. ROB-21 was added by A9 for F-e values the preprint cites that the
drafted rows do not carry.

| ESC-01 | **The registered selective-escalation hypothesis (H2b, `docs/prereg_v1.md`) is a primary null on dev: no learned gate discriminates needed from needless interventions, and the executor self-gate never fires.** On real decision points (the `scored` population) the scored AUROCs are 0.5082 / 0.4971 / 0.5000 for `router_seq_tau03/05/07` and 0.4332 / 0.3867 / 0.4407 for `sidekick_tau03/05/07`, i.e. the range **0.3867–0.5082**; the self-gate's `p_ask` never exceeds **0.0347** (maximum, `sidekick_tau03`) against operating thresholds 0.3 / 0.5 / 0.7, so all three `sidekick` arms and `router_seq_tau07` escalated zero times and are behaviourally identical to `sft_plan` [OBSERVED campaign/results/hj8_frontier_dev_20260920.report.json: keys `h3[*].{label,auroc,n_escalations}`, `h3_score_quantiles.sidekick_tau03.max`] [OBSERVED docs/prereg_j9_freeze_20260920.md:173, §6 claim 4; :33-44, §2.1]. | `campaign/results/hj8_frontier_dev_20260920.report.json` | `h3[*].auroc`; `h3_score_quantiles.*.max` | **registered (H2b)**, primary null | — |

## PROV — provenance of refilled episodes

| claim_id | claim (one sentence) | artifact path | JSON key or line | status | figure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PROV-01 | **No published campaign is proven to contain a second roll of a scored failure, and the two whose refill split cannot be recovered are bounded in the conservative direction.** Until 72899b5 / 5c331bf, resubmissions ran `--purge-broken`, which also deletes and re-runs `timeout` / `parse_error` / `api_error` episodes. An audit of every PBS log (98 worktree purge lines, 6 with N > 0; 6 in `~/.hpc-spool`, 3 with N > 0; 2026-09-23) found: (a) the refills of `hj13_planner_alone_cap81_20260923` (27), `hj13_planner_alone_cap81_seed3_20260924` (47) and `hj4_correction_train_20260917` (3) were exactly the prior job's crashes [OBSERVED campaign/workers/logs/hj13_planner_cap81.out:83-87, hj1b_planner.out:6,21,37-38]; (b) for `hj1r_exec8b_20260916`, the prior job logged no error tally. The 10 re-run episodes (`result.json` mtimes 21:32–21:35, after the other 104) now sum to 2.31 `goal_pass` and 1 solved task, so its floor (0.1903 / 0.0175) is inflated by **at most +2.03 pp `goal_pass` and +0.88 pp TGC**; (c) for `hj1r_prompt_only_20260916`, one episode re-run of unknown prior type (`50e1ac9_2` s1, mtime 2026-09-17 01:07, now 0.5 / TGC 0) bounds the inflation of its floor (0.2885 / 0.0526) at **+0.44 pp `goal_pass`, 0 TGC**. CHAN-ZS-01, TAILOR-06 and NARR-02 are gains measured over these floors, so an inflated floor can only have understated them. Some Sept 16–17 logs were overwritten, because a fixed `#PBS -o` name keeps only the last job, and PBS history no longer lists those jobs. The mtime clusters are what recover the re-run sets. | `campaign/workers/logs/`; `/scratch/n12194778/sidekick/results/hj1r_{exec8b,prompt_only}_20260916` | purge lines `[purge] removed N broken`; `result.json` mtimes and `goal_pass_rate` / `tgc` | exploratory | — |
| PROV-02 | **Six published prefix arms are not pure replay: `prefix_handoff` honours executor asks with a live planner call, and in them hosted luna answered.** `allow_executor_ask=True` (`src/sidekick/systems/prefix_handoff.py:42`) sends an `ASK_PLANNER` to `planner.correct()` live (`src/sidekick/systems/loop.py:1042-1085`). By `"event_type": "intervention"` with `usage.provider == "codex"`, every other published prefix arm has **0** such episodes, including all `hj12_prefix_*`, `hj17_*` and `hj18_*` (the headline depth curve, POOL-01..03, CHAN-C1-03). The six exceptions, each with (task, seed) episodes / live calls / bound on the arm-mean `goal_pass` lift, where the bound is Σ goal_pass of the affected episodes / n: `hj13_prefix_hf_m6`: 7 / 10 / **2.15 pp** (TGC 0.88); `hj13_prefix_hf_m9`: 3 / 3 / 0.88; `hj13_prefix_hf_m11`: 4 / 31 / 0.85 (one episode, `6171bbc_1` s2, took 27 answers); `hj14_prefix_m9`: 4 / 6 / 1.36; `hj15_prefix_zsq_m11`: 1 / 1 / 0.18; `hj19_prefix_m11_qwen_notk`: 2 / 2 / 0.35. The affected episodes score below their arm means. Some answers told the executor to stop and report it was blocked, so the effect need not be positive. Consequences: **HF-01 / HF-02:** the suffix-handoff adapter's m=6 lead (+2.43 pp) and m=9 "within 0.12 pp" do not survive the bound. The C3 "no" and every includes-zero verdict stand. **QWEN-05:** "make no live planner calls" was wrong for m11 (1 call). **QWEN-04, QWEN-06, QWEN-07:** readings unchanged. **The preprint:** paper:60-61 ("Every prefix arm in this paper *replays* a recorded planner campaign rather than calling the planner online") must be scoped to exclude these arms. paper:763 and paper:769 (the handoff adapter's shallow-end lead) must be withdrawn. The two reports that carry these arms already record the calls: `arms.handoff_m{6,9,11}.planner_calls_total` = 10 / 3 / 31 in `hj13_receiver_threeway_20260923.report.json`, and `arms.qwen_prefix_m11.planner_calls_total` = 1 in `hj15_qwen_curve_20260923.report.json`. Side finding: QWEN-06's hj14 m=9 value 0.6437 divides by 114 and so scores its one crash as 0; the non-crashed mean is 0.6494. Its reading is unchanged. The LP replay arms answer asks with their own local planner (LP prereg Amendment 2). **The gate caught this at run time and was not acted on.** `campaign_summarize --gate` printed `expected zero live planner calls but saw 10 … this arm was supposed to be free`, and `hj13_prefix_hf_m6_20260923 manifest/gate rc=1` [OBSERVED campaign/workers/logs/hj12_prefix_free_20260923.25699981.aqua.out:4145-4147]. The arm was reported anyway. | `/scratch/n12194778/sidekick/results/{hj13_prefix_hf_m{6,9,11}_20260923,hj14_prefix_m9_20260924,hj15_prefix_zsq_m11_20260923,hj19_prefix_m11_qwen_notk_20260924}` | `events.jsonl` intervention events with `usage.provider`; `result.json` `goal_pass_rate` / `tgc` | exploratory | — |

TGC: +5.26 pp, scenario [−0.58, +12.28], which includes zero [OBSERVED key `exploratory.tgc_contrasts.D0`]. This is an **extension, not a replication**: seeds 1–2 are the same 114 pairs as CHAN-C1-02 (+6.69 pp [+1.29, +13.48]), and seed 3 adds 57 whose plan packets come from the cap-81 campaign `hj13_planner_alone_cap81_seed3_20260924`, not the cap-25 one. D0 is not in B2's Holm family and carries no B2 rule. ⚠ Wording from here on: "significant under the registered percentile bootstrap, stable across seven bootstrap seeds; an exact cluster randomization test gives p = 0.063". Never "robust". | `campaign/results/b2_decomposition_20260923.report.json` | `contrasts.D0` | registered contrast, reported (extension of CHAN-C1-02) | — |
| DEC-03 | **A neutral advice prompt recovers about three-fifths of the channel gap, and against it the action channel's remaining lead is not resolved.** D3 = N − A +3.73 pp, scenario [−0.06, +8.24], task [+0.04, +7.66]; raw p 0.0548, Holm 0.2192, exact sign-flip 0.1089; POOL-04 fired (bound within 1 pp of zero), and all seven re-draws **do not exclude zero** on the positive side, so the near miss is stable, not a seed artefact [OBSERVED key `contrasts.D3`]. D4 = T − N +2.40 pp, scenario [−2.94, +8.89], task [−1.97, +7.05], Holm 0.7208 [OBSERVED key `contrasts.D4`]. On TGC the gap closes entirely: D4 −0.58 pp [−8.77, +7.02], D3 +5.85 pp [−1.17, +14.04] [OBSERVED key `exploratory.tgc_contrasts.{D3,D4}`]. 3.73 / 6.13 ≈ 0.61 [INFERRED arithmetic]. Reading: the registered advice prompt ("The executor needs a correction. Reply with concise correction text only.", `src/sidekick/agents/planner.py:118`) presumes an error and caps length; rewording it moves advice most of the way to takeover. The prompt-artefact rule missed only on D3's lower bound. So the headline cannot be stated as a channel effect without this qualification: **"actions beat the registered advice prompt; against a neutral advice prompt the difference is +2.40 pp and not resolved."** | `campaign/results/b2_decomposition_20260923.report.json` | `contrasts.D3`, `contrasts.D4` | registered contrasts (decision-bearing, not rejected); reading is interpretation | — |

Copy rate (exploratory): 38 of 228 shown actions reproduced verbatim as the executor's next action (0.1667); by kind CODE 31/213, COMPLETE 7/10, ASK_PLANNER 0/5; 152 of 171 S episodes had at least one shown action [OBSERVED key `exploratory.copy_rate`]. ## J17 — dev review fixes: handoff-only depth, chord, Ganz metrics, cost share, limits, content, metrics, latency, BY-FDR, census (A10)

Added 2026-09-24 (A10, plan R7.7) for the numbers Paper A v2 quotes from two committed dev reports:
`campaign/results/j17_depth_fixes_20260924.report.json` (built by `scripts/analysis/j17_depth_fixes.py`; HO-*,
HO-NI-*, CHORD-*, GANZ-*, COST-04) and `campaign/results/j17_channel_fixes_20260924.report.json` (built by
`scripts/analysis/j17_channel_fixes.py`; LIM-*, DEC-06, METRIC-*, LAT-01, FDR-01, CENSUS-01). **Every row is
exploratory**: these are dev re-analyses chosen after the adversarial review
(`docs/review_paperA_adversarial_20260923.md` §F1, §F2, §F4, §F7), and none decides a registered verdict. Keys are
jq paths into the report named in the artifact column. Every value is first given as the JSON holds it (the
`jq -r` output, with the JSON's ASCII minus written − and a + on positive signed quantities); where the paper
prints a shorter form, "(printed …)" follows: pp values and their intervals at 2 decimals, means, rates and
p-values at 4 decimals, ratio intervals at 2 decimals (arm-mean intervals at 4). Unless a row says otherwise,
intervals are 95 % percentile cluster-bootstrap intervals, B = 10000, seed 20260924 (the channel
report's `settings` holds B = 10000, seed 20260924), scenario clustering first (19 clusters) and
task clustering second (57 clusters); pp values are arm minus comparator over (task_id, seed)
pairs; p is the two-sided scenario-bootstrap p, and a p of 0.0 means no resample crossed the threshold. DEC-06
continues the DEC family and COST-04 the COST family.

| HO-01 | **In the pooled cap-81 prefix family the prefix leaves the executor no handoff in 4 of 171 episodes at m = 6, 54 of 171 at m = 9 and 100 of 171 at m = 11, and the zero-shot receiver's counts are the same.** Arms `hj17_prefix_c81_{bplus,zs}_m{6,9,11}_20260923` (seeds 1–2) pooled with `hj18_prefix_c81s3_{bplus,zs}_m{6,9,11}_20260924` (seed 3); 0 crashes in any input campaign [OBSERVED key `inputs.n_crash_total`]. "Silenced" (h = 0) is the prefix arm's `handoff_occurred = false`; a missing flag would count as silenced. Tailored, m6 / m9 / m11: `handoff_occurred = false` 4 / 54 / 100; executor never acted 4 / 43 / 83; `handoff_occurred = false` although the executor acted 0 / 11 / 17; flag missing 0 / 0 / 0 [OBSERVED key `handoff_only.bplus.m{6,9,11}.*`]. Zero-shot, same four counts in the same order: 4 / 54 / 100; 4 / 43 / 83; 0 / 11 / 17; 0 / 0 / 0 [OBSERVED key `handoff_only.zs.m{6,9,11}.*`]. The no-handoff counts match the review's recount from `events.jsonl` (docs/review_paperA_adversarial_20260923.md:37-42). "No handoff" and "executor never acted" differ for the reason ROB-12 records on the cap-25 source: the prefix can exhaust an episode after the executor has acted. **A11 (2026-09-24) →** The last sentence's explanation is wrong. "No handoff" (`handoff_occurred = false`) and "executor never acted" differ because the flag is `effective_m < n_source_actions` (src/sidekick/prefix_source.py:184), which is false whenever the source made no more than m executed actions, while the loop suppresses the live phase only when the replayed prefix is terminal (src/sidekick/systems/loop.py:743-756; `prefix_is_terminal` :172-180). The 11 (m = 9) and 17 (m = 11) flag-false episodes in which the executor acted are prefixes that did **not** exhaust the episode. At m = 11 all 17 sources stopped executing after 7 to 11 actions, never sent COMPLETE, and ran to the 40-step limit issuing only REPORT and ASK_PLANNER. The executor took control at step effective_m + 1 (HSTAR-14). The analysis indicator is now h* (HSTAR-01), under which silenced = "executor never acted" = 4 / 43 / 83 on both receivers (HSTAR-02); HO-02 to HO-NI-03 are superseded by HSTAR-03 to HSTAR-13 and kept as the flag sensitivity. ROB-12 gives the same explanation ("the prefix exhausted them, yet the executor still acted") for the cap-25 source. | FDR-02 | **Benjamini–Yekutieli over every paired-contrast interval printed in the abstract and §1–§9 (60 intervals) keeps 13 at q ≤ 0.05 on the scenario-clustered bootstrap p, and 4 on the exact sign-flip p. None of the channel results survives: D0 at 171 pairs, C1 at 114, the cap change CEIL-07, the limit-as-0 sensitivity and the m = 11 DiD all fail.** Fills **brief 273 / now 289** and **brief 356 / now 372** ("BY adjustment over every interval the paper prints (R4.3)"). **The set.** Candidates are the paired-contrast intervals, one per distinct (contrast, population, metric). A paired contrast is a mean of per-pair differences on (task, seed), or on (scenario, seed) units for SGC. The class includes handoff-only and silenced subsets, the parts of the step-limit split, limit-as-0, chord residuals and per-episode DiD. Excluded: ratios, shares, slopes, arm means, anything printed without an interval, and the appendices. Of the 68 candidates, **60** are printed in the main text of `paper/preprint_dev_v2_20260924.md` sha256 `6089ca93c467202141aa85f285f667dd640e1dcd252ef2510af10ed9ee73eaa7` (303 lines before "## Appendix A"). Membership is found from the file at build time. By metric: 33 `goal_pass`, 21 TGC and 6 SGC. The 16 Table 6 NI cells are tested at −7.00 pp, as FDR-01 does; everything else is tested at 0. The main text holds 139 intervals. The 22 that no candidate accounts for are all in the excluded classes (listed at the key). The 8 other candidates are now printed only in the appendices: the T − N limit split (2), H2 vs the plan floor and vs advise_k10, the zs m11 chord, narrated untailored m9 → m11, narrated − executed tailored m11, and the DiD on TGC. **p.** Two-sided, scenario-clustered percentile bootstrap (`j10_report.bootstrap_pvalue`), 10,000 draws at seed 20260924. Depth, tailored m6 → 11: `goal_pass` all 0.00624, `goal_pass` handoff-only 0.023399, TGC all 0.0, TGC silenced 0.00624. Depth, untailored m6 → 11: `goal_pass` all 0.0, TGC all 0.0. Chord c81 tailored m11 0.023399. NI `goal_pass` all at tailored m9 0.025919, tailored m11 0.0, untailored m9 0.0, untailored m11 0.0. Narrated − executed untailored m11 0.0. A BY p of 0.0 means no bootstrap resample reached the threshold [INFERRED reading]. **Sign-flip survivors, 4, each BY p 0.034276**: NI `goal_pass` all at tailored m11, untailored m9 and untailored m11, and narrated − executed untailored m11. **Casualties** (raw → BY bootstrap p): D0 0.0202 → 0.202572 (sign-flip 0.062988 → 0.631665); C1 at 114 0.0116 → 0.148054; CEIL-07 0.0106 → 0.144407; D0 limit-as-0 0.0048 → 0.089854; D0 either-limit contribution 0.0056 → 0.098277; DiD m11 `goal_pass` 0.0074 → 0.112317; untailored handoff-only m6 → 11 0.0036 → 0.072204. `goal_pass` only (m = 33): 11 survive on the bootstrap p, 7 on sign-flip. | CENSUS-02 | **The dev report JSONs hold at least 2,164 distinct paired contrasts with an interval: 8,917 contrast objects across 68 report files.** Fills **brief 376 / now 392** ("number of dev contrasts run (R4.4)"). **Rule** (verbatim at the key). Files: every `campaign/results/**/*.report.json` except those named `j10_*`, `j11_*` or `j12_*`, and this unit's own report. 3 files are excluded by name: `j10_a1_registered_dev_basis_20260924`, `j11_power_dev_20260924` and `j12_power_dev_20260924`. A contrast object is a JSON object that holds a paired-difference point (`diff_pp`, `diff`, `point_pp`, `diff_pp_scenario`, `residual_mean_pp` or `delta_pp`) and a key containing `ci95`, either at its own level or in a `scenario` / `task` sub-block. It can also be a `point` with a `ci95` key under a parent key matching one of `minus`, `_to_`, `delta`, `contribution`, `gain`, `did`, `diff` or `residual` (the report states the regex). Only the outermost such object counts. `n_objects` counts (file, key path) pairs: 8,917. `n_distinct` collapses objects whose (point, first interval), rounded to 4 dp, coincide: 2,164. **It is a lower bound.** Shares, ratios and tests printed without an interval are not counted. 13 of the 68 files contribute 0, among them the six `hj13_shape_*` hinge-fit reports, whose blocks hold β/τ intervals, not differences. The count also depends on when it is built: it includes `j17_planning_lit_20260924.report.json` (36 objects), which another unit wrote today. Printed: "at least 2,164 distinct paired contrasts (8,917 contrast objects in 68 dev report files; CENSUS-02)". | `campaign/results/j17_v2_fill_20260924.report.json` | `contrast_census.{rule,n_files,files_excluded_by_name,n_objects,n_distinct,per_file}` | exploratory (census) | — |

| TERM-01 | **Termination census comparable to Fan et al. (arXiv 2609.20804): definitions, and the no-op control that validates them.** An action is an event_type `action` of the acting actor. The executor's is written at `src/sidekick/systems/loop.py:598-605`; in planner_alone arms the planner's is written at `:998-1005`. A takeover move is the planner's (`:854-861`) and never counts. "Acted" means at least one action with `payload.kind == 'CODE'`: CODE and COMPLETE both reach `env.step` (`:1141-1143`), but COMPLETE ends the episode (`:1169-1170`). Prefix arms count only actions after the handoff, that is, step > the handoff report's `effective_m` (written at `src/sidekick/systems/prefix_handoff.py:183-189`). Categories, first rule that applies: no_handoff (prefix arm, `handoff_occurred` not true); limit; other (any other non-null error_type); stopped_before_acting (clean end, zero CODE actions); completed_after_acting [OBSERVED key `settings.definitions`]. Control: `dev_noop_complete_20260924` classifies 114/114 episodes as stopped_before_acting, share 1.0 (printed 1.0000), scenario and task [1.0, 1.0], median 1.0 steps, and all 114 actions are COMPLETE [OBSERVED key `term.arms.noop`]. Crashes dropped before classification: 0 in every census arm [OBSERVED key `provenance.*.n_crash`]. The handoff flag is read from the last `report` that carries `handoff_occurred`. j10's reader takes the last `report` from any actor; the two disagree on 0 episodes here [OBSERVED key `term.arms.*.n_handoff_flag_differs_from_j10_reader`]. | `campaign/results/j17_planning_lit_20260924.report.json` | `settings.definitions`; `term.arms.noop` | exploratory | termination census |

| HSTAR-01 | **The cap-81 prefix family's handoff indicator is now h* = "the executor took control". It is read from each prefix episode's own events, and it agrees with the loop's own terminal rule on all 1,026 prefix episodes (6 arms × 171). The `handoff_occurred` flag is kept as `h_flag`.** Rule: h* = 1 iff the last attempt in the prefix episode's `events.jsonl` holds an event that only the live loop writes, at step ≥ effective_m + 1. effective_m comes from the handoff record, a system `report` written at step effective_m (src/sidekick/systems/prefix_handoff.py:179-196). The live loop starts at `max(1, prefix.start_step)` = effective_m + 1 (loop.py:621, :753; prefix_source.py:178). Live events are: executor `action` / `error` / `ask` / `report` (loop.py:598-605, :546-580, :1053, :1087, :1112, :1130-1136); a system `error` without `exc_type` (limits :354-360, :760-766, :1176-1182); an environment `observation` / `error`, or a `local` `evaluate` (:786-792); and planner `intervention` / `action` / `handoff` / `error` / `action_review` (action_review_gate.py:77-88). Excluded: the final `evaluate` and `run_end` (:1196-1202, :1238-1245), which can sit above effective_m when the source prefix held REPORT steps, and the crash handler (:1206-1212) [OBSERVED scripts/analysis/handoff_control.py:1-60, :80, :118-141]. Validation, dev, both receivers. (1) h_flag = true ⇒ h* = 1 on 167/167, 117/117 and 71/71 at m = 6, 9, 11, with 0 violations. (2) On flag-false episodes, h* = NOT `prefix_is_terminal`, recomputed from the SOURCE episode with sidekick's own `build_handoff_prefix`, `last_observation_from_events` and `prefix_is_terminal`: m6 4 = 4 terminal + 0 live, m9 54 = 43 + 11, m11 100 = 83 + 17, 0 disagreements. These match the expected counts, and the equality holds on every episode (171/171 in all six arms). The handoff record agrees with the recomputed source on effective_m, n_source_actions, handoff_occurred and source campaign (0 mismatches in six arms), and the two receivers give identical keys. (3) A synthetic planless episode (effective_m 0) gives h* = 1. (4) A synthetic terminal episode (evaluate at step 10 > effective_m 9) gives h* = 0. | DIV-01 | **The two deterministic replay divergences on dev are false positives of the replay check. What varies between Python processes is the printed output, not the world's state.** The check hashes the cumulative execute input and printed output, not the database (`src/sidekick/environments/appworld_env.py:147-158`). DIVDIAG (PBS 25850886) replayed each key step by step in two fresh processes. `68ee2c9_2` first differs at step 11: an object repr carries a memory address. `df61dc5_2` first differs at step 10: the print order of a `set` of strings (`PYTHONHASHSEED` is unset). In all four runs the hash breaks at exactly the first step whose text differs and is equal wherever the text is equal, and the two processes also differ from each other at those steps. Controls `68ee2c9_1` and `df61dc5_3` replay hash-equal at every step to 11. A regex scan of steps ≤ 11 finds such prints in exactly those 2 of 114 Qwen3.8-27B source episodes, and in 0 of 285 `gpt-5.6-luna` source episodes (`hj13_planner_alone_cap81` seeds 1–2 and 3, `hj1b_planner`). The regex misses set order used without being printed, so the luna zero is a lower bound. | `campaign/results/replay_divergence_diagnosis_dev_20260925.json` (script `scripts/analysis/replay_divergence_diagnose.py`) | `summary.failing`; `summary.controls`; `summary.scan` | exploratory (process), dev | — |

## BFCL-DEV — the BFCL `multi_turn_base` dev read (exploratory, dev split; `docs/prereg_bfcl_dev_20260924.md` §6, no decision rule)

Added 2026-09-28 (A15) for the numbers the BFCL test E-prereg (`docs/prereg_bfcl_test_20260925.md`, FROZEN
2026-09-28) takes from the BFCL dev read, which had no ledger row before. Artifact
`campaign/results/bfcl_dev_20260924.report.json`, built by `scripts/analysis/bfcl_dev_report.py`, committed at
`8d5e8e2` with `meta.git_sha` `660a69d` and `meta.generated_at` 2026-09-28T05:17:07+00:00: 21 arms, each 50 dev
entries × seeds 1–3 = 150 episodes, all complete, 0 crashes, no campaign absent [OBSERVED key
`meta.{git_sha,generated_at,all_arms_complete,arms_incomplete,campaigns_absent,n_expected_per_arm}`]. **Every row is
exploratory and carries no decision rule**: "No dev number carries a decision rule. The E-prereg fixes the following
before any test episode: the NI margin for P3; which receiver P3 registers; the Holm families; the bootstrap seed."
(dev prereg :170-175). Each §6 contrast gets "a point estimate, a paired interval and the per-pair SD that sizes the
E-prereg's power" (:151-152). Keys are jq paths into that report, whose contrast names are defined at
`scripts/analysis/bfcl_dev_report.py:95-127`. Values are given as the JSON holds them, with − for the ASCII minus and
+ on positive signed quantities. The report already rounds pp differences and their intervals to 2 decimals and
per-pair SDs to 3, so a pp value prints as written (a trailing zero the JSON drops is restored: −18.4 prints
−18.40); means and rates, 6 decimals in the JSON, print at 4. Unless a row says otherwise, pairs are (entry, seed),
150 per contrast, 0 dropped; intervals are 95 % percentile entry-cluster bootstrap intervals over the 50 entries,
all seeds of an entry resampled together, B = 10000, seed **20260925** (not the AppWorld rows' 20260924); p is the
two-sided bootstrap p at 0 (`j10_report.bootstrap_pvalue`); SD is the per-pair SD in pp [OBSERVED keys
`meta.{n_boot,seed,cluster_unit,ci}`, `meta.definitions.{pairing,cluster,ci95_entry,sd_pp,p_two_sided}`]. Receivers
(dev prereg :59-67): `zs`, zero-shot Granite 4.2 8B, BFCL's primary receiver; `bplus`, "the AppWorld-tailored
adapter, out of domain", never "tailored" to BFCL (`docs/prereg_bfcl_test_20260925.md:181`); `qzs`, zero-shot
Qwen3-8B, a second executor family that §6 describes and never tests. Metrics: `goal_pass_rate` (primary, the share
of checked turns passing both per-turn checks) and `success` (secondary, the whole-entry verdict) (dev prereg
:27-30). Hosted calls are the ledger key minus the cached-plan records ("The dev read reports hosted calls the same
way", dev prereg :137-140; `calls_live_mean`); `calls_ledger_mean` counts a replayed plan as one call, and
`calls_attributed_mean` also charges a prefix arm its replayed prefix [OBSERVED key
`meta.definitions.{calls_live_mean,calls_ledger_mean,calls_attributed_mean}`]. POOL-04's seven-seed probe has not
been run on any bound in this section: a bound within 1 pp of zero is flagged ⚠ and not read.

| claim_id | claim (one sentence) | artifact path | JSON key or line | status | figure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BFCL-DEV-01 | **P6: on BFCL dev the action channel beats correction advice at a matched k = 5 trigger by +11.12 pp `goal_pass_rate`, entry interval [+4.16, +18.22], per-pair SD 38.055 pp.** §6: "**P6**: `takeover_k5 − advise_k5_fullctx`. Does the action channel beat correction advice at a matched trigger?" (dev prereg :153).
